### 1. Additional work on PES schemes is nedeed

Over the years, PES schemes became an important tool for natural ressources’ conservation. In Europe, PES are discussed in the context of the of the post-2020 Common Agricultural Policy (CAP) reform^38. In France, PES development is discussed at the political level : in a recent amendment studied by the french senate^39, PES are presented as « a tool that can combine economic necessity with societal expectations ». In the academic literature, analyses of PES schemes seem to heavily rely on emblematic cases such as the schemes implemented in New-York, in Costa Rica and on the Vittel watershed. The vittel case is particularly interesting, as it can be labelled as a Cosean PES i.e. « [PES] that emmerge from contractual transactions between private actors (i.e., providers and users of a service) in the absence of overt government intervention » (Bingham, 2021). These programs are also called “user- financed” or “privately-funded” PES (Bingham, 2021 ; Ezzine-de-Blas et al. 2016). In France, it represents almost one-third of the PES schemes (32%) (Duval et al. 2019). The Vittel case study is probably the most emblematic french private case study (Claise et al. 2022), even if references to other schemes exist (Chevrier et al. 2017). Indeed Bingham (2021) reported 72 peer-reviewed journal articles in english over the period 2010-2020, that mentionned the Vittel case. However, he stated that these references « are often more didacticthan analytic: because it is often cited as a famous, easy-to-understand illustration of a Coasean PES solution, detailed, in-depth assessments are uncommon ». Some initiatives are nevertheless noticable: an interesting study by Grémillet and Fosse (2020) assess different agri-environmental « cahiers des charges » using the scoring method upon various criterias relatives to biodiversity, ressources preservation, fertilizers use etc. Nevertheless, additional work is still needed for decision-makers interrested in developping a PES program, in order to have in mind how large and diversify total benefits from such schemes can be. This thesis participates to fill this gap. [SOURCE: Page_259]

39 See: https://www.senat.fr/amendements/2020-2021/667/Amdt_1668.html [SOURCE: Page_259]

### 2. Objective of the thesis

The aim of this thesis project was to investigate the co-benefits of the Vittel PES i.e. benefits that are additional to water quality. This work is in line with recent publications investigating secondary benefits of nature-based solution schemes (Le Coent et al. 2023 ; Hérivaux and Le Coent, 2021; Hérivaux et Grémont, 2019) and in particular Hérivaux and Le Coent (2021) who implemented a DCE that evaluates to assess preferences for various types of NBS relative to flood risks. As the authors suggested, this kind of analysis is applicable to others topics than flood risks, such as the protection of water ressources. In this thesis, I applied NMV methods (DCE and HPM) to value the different co-benefits associated with the Vittel PSE program. [SOURCE: Page_260]

### 3. Main results

In chapter one, I conducted a DCE to investigate the preferences of the local population around the Vittel impluvium: the idea was to test different scenarios of recreational activities. Each scenario (or alternative) was composed of attributes representing common features of recreational sites (picnic tables ect.) and more specific attributes related to agri-environmental measures implemented on the Vittel watershed (nonuse of pesticides, for instance). I gave respondents the choice between three alternatives: two hypothetical sites (forest or countryside because they are common areas for recreation) and one alternative representing the last forest or countryside visited by the respondent. Some attributes were common to each area (water course, for example) and others were specific to the forest (the type of forest) or to the countryside (such as the agricultural land use). Each individual faced eight different choice sets (combinaison of three alternatives). It allowed me to analyse the trade-offs made by respondents between the characteristics of the recreational sites. I have found that respondents expressed preferences for some environmental characteristics, like the presence of a water courses or the nonuse of pesticides, which is an important measure in the Vittel impluvium. The marginal willignesses to pay (MWTP) for environmental characteristics reflected this trend: people were ready to pay significant amounts to go to sites were pesticides were not used, but overall, preferences for environmental attributes were quite heterogenous. [SOURCE: Page_260]

In chapter two, I implemented an other DCE that aimed, this time, to target nonusers of water ressources. NUV can be substantial for some environmental assets (Marre et al. 2015). To make the DCE exercice more realistic, we informed respondents that environmental preservation projects (including measures for banning pesticides, restoring water courses etc.) [SOURCE: Page_260]

258 [SOURCE: Page_260]

were about to be implemented in the Rupt de Mad watershed located in the Rhin-Meuse Basin. I identified residents consuming drinking water from this watershed in order to exclude them from the final sample. Individuals who answered the DCE were located on the french territory, sometimes really far from the watershed. It allowed me to investigate the existence of a distance-decay effect i.e. the idea that the further individuals were from the Rupt de Mad watershed, the less concerned they felt about environmental improvements. To do so, i used the centroids of the respondent's municipality of residence to calculate distances to the watershed. [SOURCE: Page_261]

Results from the econometric analysis have shown that preferences for environmental attributes were quite heterogeneous. I estimated a LCM with four classes. The objective was to identify different profiles of respondents classes: individuals in class one are willing to pay a significant amount per year (€10.69) on the water bill to ban pesticides. Respondents from the second class had high WTP for some environmental measures (banning pesticides, restoring watercourses, planting hedges and managing forests). Nevertheless, these WTP were estimated with a large confidence interval, due to weakly significant coefficient on the monetary attribue. The third class gathered individuals that were the closest to the site of interest. In particular, they were willing to pay a very high amount (€133.72) for pesticide ban. Finally, people in class 4 had only positive preferences for pesticide ban and watercourse restoration. In opposition to class two, their WTP were quite low (€3.48 and €4.60, respectively for pesticides ban and watercourse restoration). [SOURCE: Page_261]

Identification of a potential distance-decay effect was not conclusive: I found that distance to environmental projects matters only for a small proportion of respondents (16%) who were allocated in the third class. [SOURCE: Page_261]

In comparison to chapter one, pesticide ban seems to be an important environmental measure for nonusers, as it was the case for individuals located around the Vittel watershed and going recreational areas. These results tend to indicate that French water authorities should prioritise pesticide bans as a measure to protect water and biodiversity, in order to be in line with public preferences. This is also consistent with the biodiversity plan presented in 2018 (MTES, 2018). [SOURCE: Page_261]

In the third chapter, i conducted an hedonic analysis on the Vosges department. I used data from the french notarial society Perval, that proved to be useful in such analyses (Osseni et al. 2021): housing characteristics (selling price without taxes, number of rooms, number of [SOURCE: Page_261]

259 [SOURCE: Page_261]

bathrooms etc.) and geographic coordinates were used in the hedonic analysis for the 1998- 2021 period. After cleaning, 10 801 observations remained in the sample. Besides house characteristics, the objective of the paper was to assess the influence of different land uses over house prices. I defined five broad categories: agriculture, forest, meadows, urban zones and others (that gathered all the remaining land uses). Two different specifications were defined (Osland et al. 2022): first, I looked at the influence of distance from houses to amenities. Secondly, I investigated the influence of the land use shares on house prices, using different buffer areas. (Liljenstolpe, 2011; Ready and Abdalla, 2005) Hedonic regressions were estimated using the ppmlhdfe Stata command (Correia et al. 2020). Econometric results that grasslands significantly inluence house prices for all the buffers considered. This is in line with what I found with the distance specification : the shorter the distance is, the higher the house price is. Overall, results were less significant for houses located inside the Vittel impluvium. Nevertheless, a positive effect of agriculture is observed in the area with the 1km buffer. It tends to indicate that within the 1km radius, positive effects of agriculture in the impluvium (absence of pesticides, landscape diversification etc.) overtake potential disamenities. [SOURCE: Page_262]

These results complete the findings of the first paper where preferences for mixed agricultural plots were found. In complement with the hedonic analysis, it seems that landscape diversification is appreciated. [SOURCE: Page_262]

Finally, the last chapter provides a data production relative to the different surveys implemented for the thesis : the two DCE (chapters 1 and 2) and a third survey relative to tourists’ preferences for forest and agricultural landscape management and the impact on the local economy in the Vittel region. This last survey complements what has been done in chapter one and chapter two for the local and non-local pupulations This data provide a description and various descriptive statistics for each survey. Data coming from these surveys are stored in the are stored in https://entrepot.recherche.data.gouv.fr/ and available in the BETA collection https://entrepot.recherche.data.gouv.fr/dataverse.xhtml?alias=PHD-BETA. It participates to the open science dynamic (Dedieu, 2022). [SOURCE: Page_262]

### 4. Limitations and perspectives

Even if the results from the four chapters appear to be quite complementary, It’s important to emphasize on several limitations. [SOURCE: Page_263]

First, the objective of this thesis was to assess the co-benefits induced by a PES implementation in the Vittel watershed. If monetary valuation exercises are useful, they need to be combined with other types of valuation (O’Hara, 2001; Odum and Odum, 2000). In particular, Hérivaux and Le Coent (2021) advice to combine monetary valuation approaches with socio-cultural valuation ones. They even propose to include a specific socio-cultural section in stated preference surveys, such as DCE. That was not the case in this study. Moreover, the method used in the different chapters are subject to various criticisms: for example, stated preference surveys can display hypothetical biais, and revealed preference methods can only capture use values. Acknowledging that, it is even more important to combine different techniques, like in this manuscript, but going even further is necessary. [SOURCE: Page_263]

Secondly, If assessing co-benefits of NBS solutions is important, reserchears should also have a look at potential negative effects of such schemes (Hérivaux and Le Coent, 2021). As I tried to assess the co-benefits of the Vittel scheme, the negative impacts are not the focus of this study. As the Vittel case study is sometimes seen as exemplary, a critical perspective is even more important. [SOURCE: Page_263]

In the same idea, the thesis does not provide a CBA of the Vittel PES. Some studies (Depres et al. 2008; Perrot-Maître, 2006) provided a cost estimation of the Vittel programm: the total cost was estimated at 24.25 million euros for Nestlé Waters for the first seven years, i.e. 980€/ha/year. It corresponds to 1.52€/m³ of bottled water produced and includes land acquisition or investments in farm equipement, for instance. But the costs linked to the creation of Agrivair are not included in that estimation (Depres et al. 2008; Perrot-Maitre, 2006). They need to be taken into account, as well as indirect costs (such as potential negative effects of the scheme) (Capodaglio and Callegari, 2018) to provide a complete CBA analysis. [SOURCE: Page_263]

Because the PES started to be implemented in the 1980’s, this thesis provides a kind of ex- post valuation process of a particular PES program. Valuation of potential benefits and costs should nevertheless be conducted ex-ante in order to take more informed decisions (Banque des territoires, 2022). That’s why we can see this work as a first step to more systematic NMV of PES schemes, in order to provide decision-makers tools to decide if they constitute the best instrument for environmental preservation. 261 [SOURCE: Page_263]
